\noindent\textbf{Q.36} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a network path P---Q---R between nodes P and R via router Q. Node P sends a file of size $10^6$ bytes to R via this path by splitting the file into chunks of $10^3$ bytes each. Node P sends these chunks one after the other without any wait time between the successive chunk transmissions. Assume that the size of extra headers added to these chunks is negligible, and that the chunk size is less than the MTU.
\par\smallskip
Each of the links P---Q and Q---R has a bandwidth of $10^6$ bits/sec, and negligible propagation latency. Router Q immediately transmits every packet it receives from P to R, with negligible processing and queueing delays. Router Q can simultaneously receive on link P---Q and transmit on link Q---R.
\par\smallskip
Assume P starts transmitting the chunks at time $t = 0$.
Which one of the following options gives the time (in seconds, rounded off to 3 decimal places) at which R receives all the chunks of the file? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~8.000 & \textbf{(B)}~8.008 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~15.992 & \textbf{(D)}~16.000
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.37} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following syntax-directed definition (SDD):
\begin{align*}
S &\to DHTU & \{ S.val = D.val + H.val + T.val + U.val; \} \\
D &\to \text{``M''} D_1 & \{ D.val = 5 + D_1.val; \} \\
D &\to \epsilon & \{ D.val = -5; \} \\
H &\to \text{``L''} H_1 & \{ H.val = 5 * 10 + H_1.val; \} \\
H &\to \epsilon & \{ H.val = -10; \} \\
T &\to \text{``C''} T_1 & \{ T.val = 5 * 100 + T_1.val; \} \\
T &\to \epsilon & \{ T.val = -5; \} \\
U &\to \text{``K''} & \{ U.val = 5; \}
\end{align*}
Given ``MMLK'' as the input, which one of the following options is the CORRECT value computed by the SDD (in the attribute $S.val$)? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~45 & \textbf{(B)}~50 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~55 & \textbf{(D)}~65
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.38} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following grammar $G$, with $S$ as the start symbol. The grammar $G$ has three incomplete productions denoted by (1), (2), and (3).
\begin{align*}
S &\to daT \mid (1) \\
T &\to aS \mid bT \mid (2) \\
R &\to (3) \mid \epsilon
\end{align*}
The set of terminals is $\{a, b, c, d, f\}$. The FIRST and FOLLOW sets of the different non-terminals are as follows:
$$\text{FIRST}(S) = \{c, d, f\}, \quad \text{FIRST}(T) = \{a, b, \epsilon\}, \quad \text{FIRST}(R) = \{c, \epsilon\}$$
$$\text{FOLLOW}(S) = \text{FOLLOW}(T) = \{c, f, \$\}, \quad \text{FOLLOW}(R) = \{f\}$$
Which one of the following options CORRECTLY fills in the incomplete productions? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~(1) $S \to Rf$ (2) $T \to \epsilon$ (3) $R \to cTR$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~(1) $S \to fR$ (2) $T \to \epsilon$ (3) $R \to cTR$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~(1) $S \to fR$ (2) $T \to cT$ (3) $R \to cR$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~(1) $S \to Rf$ (2) $T \to cT$ (3) $R \to cR$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.39} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following pseudo-code:
\begin{lstlisting}
L1:  t1 = -1
L2:  t2 = 0
L3:  t3 = 0
L4:  t4 = 4 * t3
L5:  t5 = 4 * t2
L6:  t6 = t5 * M
L7:  t7 = t4 + t6
L8:  t8 = a[t7]
L9:  if t8 <= max goto L11
L10: t1 = t8
L11: t3 = t3 + 1
L12: if t3 < M goto L4
L13: t2 = t2 + 1
L14: if t2 < N goto L3
L15: max = t1
\end{lstlisting}
Which one of the following options CORRECTLY specifies the number of basic blocks and the number of instructions in the largest basic block, respectively? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~6 and 6 & \textbf{(B)}~6 and 7 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~7 and 7 & \textbf{(D)}~7 and 6
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.40} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following two threads T1 and T2 that update two shared variables `a` and `b`. Assume that initially $a = b = 1$. Though context switching between threads can happen at any time, each statement of T1 or T2 is executed atomically without interruption.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{p{4cm}p{4cm}}
\textbf{T1} & \textbf{T2} \\
\texttt{a = a + 1;} & \texttt{b = 2 * b;} \\
\texttt{b = b + 1;} & \texttt{a = 2 * a;}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Which one of the following options lists all the possible combinations of values of `a` and `b` after both T1 and T2 finish execution? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$(a = 4, b = 4)$; $(a = 3, b = 3)$; $(a = 4, b = 3)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$(a = 3, b = 4)$; $(a = 4, b = 3)$; $(a = 3, b = 3)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$(a = 4, b = 4)$; $(a = 4, b = 3)$; $(a = 3, b = 4)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$(a = 2, b = 2)$; $(a = 2, b = 3)$; $(a = 3, b = 4)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.41} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries An array $[82, 101, 90, 11, 111, 75, 33, 131, 44, 93]$ is heapified. Which one of the following options represents the first three elements in the heapified array? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~82, 90, 101 & \textbf{(B)}~82, 11, 93 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~131, 11, 93 & \textbf{(D)}~131, 111, 90
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.42} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following recurrence relation:
\[ T(n) = \begin{cases} \sqrt{n} T(\sqrt{n}) + n & \text{for } n > 1, \\ 1 & \text{for } n = 1. \end{cases} \]
Which one of the following options is CORRECT? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n \log \log n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n \log n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n^2 \log n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n^2 \log \log n)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.43} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a binary min-heap containing 105 distinct elements. Let $k$ be the index (in the underlying array) of the maximum element stored in the heap. The number of possible values of $k$ is \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~53 & \textbf{(B)}~52 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~27 & \textbf{(D)}~1
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.44} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The symbol $\to$ indicates functional dependency in the context of a relational database. Which of the following options is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$(X, Y) \to (Z, W)$ implies $X \to (Z, W)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$(X, Y) \to (Z, W)$ implies $(X, Y) \to Z$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$((X, Y) \to Z \text{ and } W \to Y)$ implies $(X, W) \to Z$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$(X \to Y \text{ and } Y \to Z)$ implies $X \to Z$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.45} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $G$ be a directed graph and $T$ a depth first search (DFS) spanning tree in $G$ that is rooted at a vertex $v$. Suppose $T$ is also a breadth first search (BFS) tree in $G$, rooted at $v$. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE for every such graph $G$ and tree $T$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~There are no back-edges in $G$ with respect to the tree $T$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~There are no cross-edges in $G$ with respect to the tree $T$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~There are no forward-edges in $G$ with respect to the tree $T$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~The only edges in $G$ are the edges in $T$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.46} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following read-write schedule $S$ over three transactions $T_1, T_2$, and $T_3$, where the subscripts in the schedule indicate transaction IDs:
\[ S: r\_1(z); w\_1(z); r\_2(x); r\_3(y); w\_3(y); r\_2(y); w\_2(x); w\_2(y); \]
Which of the following transaction schedules is/are conflict equivalent to $S$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$T_1 T_2 T_3$ & \textbf{(B)}~$T_1 T_3 T_2$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$T_3 T_2 T_1$ & \textbf{(D)}~$T_3 T_1 T_2$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.47} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a Boolean expression given by $F(X, Y, Z) = \sum(3, 5, 6, 7)$. Which of the following statements is/are CORRECT? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$F(X, Y, Z) = \prod(0, 1, 2, 4)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$F(X, Y, Z) = XY + YZ + XZ$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$F(X, Y, Z)$ is independent of input $Y$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$F(X, Y, Z)$ is independent of input $X$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.48} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following C function definition:
\begin{lstlisting}[language=C]
int f(int x, int y) {
    for (int i = 0; i < y; i++) {
        x = x + x + y;
    }
    return x;
}
\end{lstlisting}
Which of the following statements is/are TRUE about the above function? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~If the inputs are $x=20, y=10$, then the return value is greater than $2^{20}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~If the inputs are $x=20, y=20$, then the return value is greater than $2^{20}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~If the inputs are $x=20, y=10$, then the return value is less than $2^{10}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~If the inputs are $x=10, y=20$, then the return value is greater than $2^{20}$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.49} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $A$ be any $n \times m$ matrix, where $m > n$. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE about the system of linear equations $Ax = 0$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~There exist at least $m-n$ linearly independent solutions to this system\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~There exist $m-n$ linearly independent vectors such that every solution is a linear combination of these vectors\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~There exists a non-zero solution in which at least $m-n$ variables are 0\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~There exists a solution in which at least $n$ variables are non-zero
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.50} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the 5-state DFA $M$ accepting the language $L(M) \subset (0 + 1)^*$ shown below. For any string $w \in (0 + 1)^*$ let $n_0(w)$ be the number of 0's in $w$ and $n_1(w)$ be the number of 1's in $w$.
Which of the following statements is/are FALSE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~States 2 and 4 are distinguishable in $M$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~States 3 and 4 are distinguishable in $M$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~States 2 and 5 are distinguishable in $M$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Any string $w$ with $n_0(w) = n_1(w)$ is in $L(M)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.51} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The chromatic number of a graph is the minimum number of colours used in a proper colouring of the graph. Let $G$ be any graph with $n$ vertices and chromatic number $k$. Which of the following statements is/are always TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$G$ contains a complete subgraph with $k$ vertices\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$G$ contains an independent set of size at least $n/k$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$G$ contains at least $k(k-1)/2$ edges\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$G$ contains a vertex of degree at least $k$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.52} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the operators $\diamond$ and $\square$ defined by $a \diamond b = a + 2b$, $a \square b = ab$, for positive integers. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Operator $\diamond$ obeys the associative law\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Operator $\square$ obeys the associative law\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Operator $\diamond$ over the operator $\square$ obeys the distributive law\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Operator $\square$ over the operator $\diamond$ obeys the distributive law
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.53} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider two set-associative cache memory architectures: WBC, which uses the write back policy, and WTC, which uses the write through policy. Both of them use the LRU (Least Recently Used) block replacement policy. The cache memory is connected to the main memory. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~A read miss in WBC never evicts a dirty block\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~A read miss in WTC never triggers a write back operation of a cache block to main memory\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~A write hit in WBC can modify the value of the dirty bit of a cache block\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~A write miss in WTC always writes the victim cache block to main memory before loading the missed block to the cache
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.54} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a 512 GB hard disk with 32 storage surfaces. There are 4096 sectors per track and each sector holds 1024 bytes of data. The number of cylinders in the hard disk is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.55} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The baseline execution time of a program on a 2 GHz single core machine is 100 nanoseconds (ns). The code corresponding to 90\% of the execution time can be fully parallelized. The overhead for using an additional core is 10 ns when running on a multicore system. Assume that all cores in the multicore system run their share of the parallelized code for an equal amount of time.
The number of cores that minimize the execution time of the program is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.56} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A given program has 25\% load/store instructions. Suppose the ideal CPI (cycles per instruction) without any memory stalls is 2. The program exhibits 2\% miss rate on instruction cache and 8\% miss rate on data cache. The miss penalty is 100 cycles.
The speedup (rounded off to two decimal places) achieved with a perfect cache (i.e., with NO data or instruction cache misses) is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in numeric format) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.57} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following code snippet using the `fork()` and `wait()` system calls. Assume that the code compiles and runs correctly, and that the system calls run successfully without any errors.
\begin{lstlisting}[language=C]
int x = 3;
while(x > 0) {
    fork();
    printf("hello");
    wait(NULL);
    x--;
}
\end{lstlisting}
The total number of times the `printf` statement is executed is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.58} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the entries shown below in the forwarding table of an IP router. Each entry consists of an IP prefix and the corresponding next hop router for packets whose destination IP address matches the prefix. The notation ``/N'' in a prefix indicates a subnet mask with the most significant $N$ bits set to 1.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{|l|c|}
\hline
\textbf{Prefix} & \textbf{Next hop router} \\
\hline
10.1.1.0/24 & R1 \\
10.1.1.128/25 & R2 \\
10.1.1.64/26 & R3 \\
10.1.1.192/26 & R4 \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
This router forwards 20 packets each to 5 hosts. The IP addresses of the hosts are 10.1.1.16, 10.1.1.72, 10.1.1.132, 10.1.1.191, and 10.1.1.205. The number of packets forwarded via the next hop router R2 is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.59} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $G = (V, \Sigma, S, P)$ be a context-free grammar in Chomsky Normal Form with $\Sigma = \{a, b, c\}$ and $V$ containing 10 variable symbols including the start symbol $S$. The string $w = a^{30}b^{30}c^{30}$ is derivable from $S$. The number of steps (application of rules) in the derivation $S \Rightarrow^* w$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.60} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The number of edges present in the forest generated by the DFS traversal of an undirected graph $G$ with 100 vertices is 40. The number of connected components in $G$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.61} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following two regular expressions over the alphabet $\{0,1\}$:
\[ r = 0^* + 1^* \]
\[ s = 01^* + 10^* \]
The total number of strings of length less than or equal to 5, which are neither in $r$ nor in $s$, is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.62} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a memory management system that uses a page size of 2 KB. Assume that both the physical and virtual addresses start from 0. Assume that the pages 0, 1, 2, and 3 are stored in the page frames 1, 3, 2, and 0, respectively. The physical address (in decimal format) corresponding to the virtual address 2500 (in decimal format) is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.63} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A bag contains 10 red balls and 15 blue balls. Two balls are drawn randomly without replacement. Given that the first ball drawn is red, the probability (rounded off to 3 decimal places) that both balls drawn are red is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in numeric format) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.65} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider sending an IP datagram of size 1420 bytes (including 20 bytes of IP header) from a sender to a receiver over a path of two links with a router between them. The first link (sender to router) has an MTU (Maximum Transmission Unit) size of 542 bytes, while the second link (router to receiver) has an MTU size of 360 bytes. The number of fragments that would be delivered at the receiver is \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\end{multicols}

\noindent\textbf{\large\underline{GENERAL APTITUDE}}\\[2pt]
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{[Section Block: 1-mark questions]}}
\vspace{4pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.1} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries If `$\to$' denotes increasing order of intensity, then the meaning of the words $[\text{walk} \to \text{jog} \to \text{sprint}]$ is analogous to $[\text{bothered} \to \text{\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_} \to \text{daunted}]$.
Which one of the given options is appropriate to fill the blank? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~phased & \textbf{(B)}~phrased \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~fazed & \textbf{(D)}~fused
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.2} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Two wizards try to create a spell using all the four elements, water, air, fire, and earth. For this, they decide to mix all these elements in all possible orders. They also decide to work independently. After trying all possible combination of elements, they conclude that the spell does not work.
How many attempts does each wizard make before coming to this conclusion, independently? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~24 & \textbf{(B)}~48 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~16 & \textbf{(D)}~12
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.3} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries In an engineering college of 10,000 students, 1,500 like neither their core branches nor other branches. The number of students who like their core branches is 1/4th of the number of students who like other branches. The number of students who like both their core and other branches is 500.
The number of students who like their core branches is: \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~1,800 & \textbf{(B)}~3,500 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~1,600 & \textbf{(D)}~1,500
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.4} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\[ \ln\left(\frac{x+y}{2}\right) = \frac{1}{2} [\ln(x) + \ln(y)] \]

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}

\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~2 & \textbf{(D)}~4
\end{tabular*}

\par\vspace{2pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.6} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Sequence the following sentences in a coherent passage.
\begin{itemize}
\item[P:] This fortuitous geological event generated a colossal amount of energy and heat that resulted in the rocks rising to an average height of 4 km across the contact zone.
\item[Q:] Thus, the geophysicists tend to think of the Himalayas as an active geological event rather than as a static geological feature.
\item[R:] The natural process of the cooling of this massive edifice absorbed large quantities of atmospheric carbon dioxide, altering the earth’s atmosphere and making it better suited for life.
\item[S:] Many millennia ago, a breakaway chunk of bedrock from the Antarctic Plate collided with the massive Eurasian Plate.
\end{itemize} \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~QPSR & \textbf{(B)}~QSPR \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~SPRQ & \textbf{(D)}~SRPQ
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.11} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a computer with a 4 MHz processor. Its DMA controller can transfer 8 bytes in 1 cycle from a device to main memory through cycle stealing at regular intervals. Which one of the following is the data transfer rate (in bits per second) of the DMA controller if 1\% of the processor cycles are used for DMA? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~2,56,000 & \textbf{(B)}~3,200 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~25,60,000 & \textbf{(D)}~32,000
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.12} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $p$ and $q$ be the following propositions:
$p$: Fail grade can be given.
$q$: Student scores more than 50\% marks.
Consider the statement: ``Fail grade cannot be given when student scores more than 50\% marks.''
Which one of the following is the CORRECT representation of the above statement in propositional logic? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$q \to \neg p$ & \textbf{(B)}~$q \to p$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$p \to q$ & \textbf{(D)}~$\neg p \to q$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.13} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following C program. Assume parameters to a function are evaluated from right to left.
\begin{verbatim}
#include <stdio.h>
int g(int p) { printf("%d", p); return p; }
int h(int q) { printf("%d", q); return q; }
void f(int x, int y) {
    g(x);
    h(y);
}
int main() {
    f(g(10), h(20));
}
\end{verbatim}
Which one of the following is the output of the program? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~20101020 & \textbf{(B)}~10202010 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~20102010 & \textbf{(D)}~10201020
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\textbf{(D)}~Sign: 0, Exponent: 0111 1111, Mantissa: 0000 0000 0000 0000 0000 000
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.15} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $T(n)$ be the recurrence relation defined as follows:
\[ T(0) = 1, \quad T(1) = 2, \quad \text{and} \quad T(n) = 5T(n-1) - 6T(n-2) \quad \text{for } n \ge 2 \]
Which one of the following statements is TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$T(n) = \Theta(2^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n 2^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$T(n) = \Theta(3^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n 3^n)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.23} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The destination IP address is the IP address of R\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~The destination IP address is the IP address of Y\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The destination MAC address is the MAC address of S\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent

\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.24} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Allocate a new page table for a newly created process\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Translate a virtual address to a physical address using the page table\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Raise a trap when a virtual address is not found in the page table\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Raise a trap when a process tries to write to a page marked with read-only permission in the page table
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.25} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~P makes a blocking system call to read a block of data from the disk\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~P tries to access a page that is in the swap space, triggering a page fault\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~An interrupt is raised by the disk to deliver data requested by some other process\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~A timer interrupt is raised by the hardware
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.27} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~2PL permits only serializable schedules\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~With 2PL, a transaction always locks the data item being read or written just before every operation\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~With 2PL, once a lock is released on any data item inside a transaction, no more locks on any data item can be acquired\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~A deadlock is possible with 2PL
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.28} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which of the following statements about IPv4 fragmentation is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The fragmentation of an IP datagram is performed only at the source of the datagram\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~The fragmentation of an IP datagram is performed at any IP router which finds that the size of the datagram is greater than the MTU of the outgoing link\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The reassembly of fragments is performed only at the destination of the datagram\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~The reassembly of fragments is performed at all intermediate routers along the path from the source to destination
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.29} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which of the following statements is/are FALSE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~An attribute grammar is a syntax-directed definition (SDD) in which the functions in the semantic rules have no side effects\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~The attributes in a L-attributed definition cannot always be evaluated in a depth-first order\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Synthesized attributes can be evaluated by a bottom-up parser as the input is parsed\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~All L-attributed definitions based on LR(1) grammar can be evaluated using a bottom-up parsing strategy
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.30} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\textbf{(B)}~$x + 1 = x$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$x \cdot x = 0$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$x + \overline{x} = 1$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.31} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries An instruction format has the following structure:
Instruction Number: Opcode destination reg, source reg-1, source reg-2
Consider the following sequence of instructions to be executed in a pipelined processor:
\begin{verbatim}

I2: SUB R5, R3, R4
I3: ADD R3, R5, R6
I4: MUL R7, R3, R8
\end{verbatim}
Which of the following dependencies is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~There is a RAW dependency on R3 between I1 and I2\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~There is a WAR dependency on R3 between I1 and I3\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~There is a RAW dependency on R3 between I2 and I3\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~There is a WAW dependency on R3 between I3 and I4
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.32} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~Source IP Address & \textbf{(B)}~Protocol \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~Time to Live (TTL) & \textbf{(D)}~Header Checksum
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.33} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following C function definition:
\begin{verbatim}
int fX(char *a){
    char *b = a;
    while(*b)
        b++;
    return b - a;
}
\end{verbatim}
Which of the following statements is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The function call fX("abcd") will always return a value\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Assuming a character array c is declared as char c[] = "abcd" in main(), the function call fX(c) will always return a value\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The code of the function will not compile\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Assuming a character pointer c is declared as char *c = "abcd" in main(), the function call fX(c) will always return a value
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.34} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $P$ be the partial order defined on the set $\{1,2,3,4\}$ as follows:
\[ P = \{(x, x) \mid x \in \{1,2,3,4\}\} \cup \{(1,2), (3,2), (3,4)\} \]
The number of total orders on $\{1,2,3,4\}$ that contain $P$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.35} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $A$ be an array containing integer values. The distance of $A$ is defined as the minimum number of elements in $A$ that must be replaced with another integer so that the resulting array is sorted in non-decreasing order. The distance of the array $[2, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2, 6]$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\vspace{6pt}
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{[Section Block: 2-mark questions]}}
\vspace{3pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.36} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries What is the output of the following C program?
\begin{verbatim}
#include <stdio.h>
int main() {
    double a[2] = {20.0, 25.0}, *p, *q;
    p = a;
    q = p + 1;
    printf("%d,%d", (int)(q - p), (int)(*q - *p));
    return 0;
}
\end{verbatim} \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~4,8 & \textbf{(B)}~1,5 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~8,5 & \textbf{(D)}~1,8
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.37} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a single processor system with four processes A, B, C, and D, represented as given below, where for each process the first value is its arrival time, and the second value is its CPU burst time:
A (0, 10), B (2, 6), C (4, 3), and D (6, 7).
Which one of the following options gives the average waiting time for Shortest Remaining Time First (SRTF) and Non-Preemptive Shortest Job First (NP-SJF) scheduling? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~SRTF = 6, NP-SJF = 7\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~SRTF = 6, NP-SJF = 7.5\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~SRTF = 7, NP-SJF = 7.5\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~SRTF = 7, NP-SJF = 8.5
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.38} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which one of the following CIDR prefixes exactly represents the range of IP addresses 10.12.2.0 to 10.12.3.255? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~10.12.2.0/23 & \textbf{(B)}~10.12.2.0/24 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~10.12.0.0/22 & \textbf{(D)}~10.12.2.0/22
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.39} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries You are given a set $V$ of distinct integers. A binary search tree $T$ is created by inserting all elements of $V$ one by one, starting with an empty tree. The tree $T$ follows the convention that, at each node, all values stored in the left subtree of the node are smaller than the value stored at the node. Which of the following is TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Inorder traversal of $T$ can be determined from $V$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Root node of $T$ can be determined from $V$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Preorder traversal of $T$ can be determined from $V$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Postorder traversal of $T$ can be determined from $V$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.40} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following context-free grammar where the start symbol is $S$ and the set of terminals is $\{a,b,c,d\}$:
\[ S \to AaAb \mid BbBa \]
\[ A \to cS \mid \epsilon \]
\[ B \to dS \mid \epsilon \]
The following is a partially-filled LL(1) parsing table:
\par\smallskip
\begin{tabular}{|c|c|c|c|c|c|}
\hline
& $a$ & $b$ & $c$ & $d$ & \$ \\
\hline
$S$ & $S \to AaAb$ & $S \to BbBa$ & (1) & (2) & \\
$A$ & $A \to \epsilon$ & (3) & $A \to cS$ & & \\
$B$ & (4) & $B \to \epsilon$ & & $B \to dS$ & \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\par\smallskip
Which one of the following options represents the CORRECT combination for the numbered cells in the parsing table? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~(1) $S \to AaAb$ (2) $S \to BbBa$ (3) $A \to \epsilon$ (4) $B \to \epsilon$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~(1) $S \to BbBa$ (2) $S \to AaAb$ (3) $A \to \epsilon$ (4) $B \to \epsilon$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~(1) $S \to AaAb$ (2) $S \to BbBa$ (3) blank (4) blank\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~(1) $S \to BbBa$ (2) $S \to AaAb$ (3) blank (4) blank
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.42} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider an array X that contains n positive integers. A subarray of X is defined to be a sequence of array locations with consecutive indices.
The C code snippet given below has been written to compute the length of the longest subarray of X that contains at most two distinct integers. The code has two missing expressions labelled $(P)$ and $(Q)$:
\begin{verbatim}
int first=0, second=0, len1=0, len2=0, maxlen=0;
for (int i=0; i < n; i++) {
    if (X[i] == first) {
        len2++; len1++;
    } else if (X[i] == second) {
        len2++;
        len1 = (P);
        second = first;
    } else {
        len2 = (Q);
        len1 = 1; second = first;
    }
    if (len2 > maxlen) maxlen = len2;
    first = X[i];
}
\end{verbatim}
Which one of the following options gives the CORRECT missing expressions? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~(P) len1+1 (Q) len2+1 & \textbf{(B)}~(P) 1 (Q) len1+1 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~(P) 1 (Q) len2+1 & \textbf{(D)}~(P) len2+1 (Q) len1+1
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.43} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following expression: $x[i] = (p + r) * -s[i] + u / w$. The following sequence shows the list of triples representing the given expression, with entries missing for triples (1), (3), and (6):
\begin{verbatim}
(0) + p r
(1)
(2) uminus (1)
(3)
(4) / u w
(5) + (3) (4)
(6)
(7) = (6) (5)
\end{verbatim}
Which one of the following options fills in the missing entries CORRECTLY? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~(1) =[] s i; (3) * (0) (2); (6) []= x i\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~(1) []= s i; (3) - (0) (2); (6) =[] x (5)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~(1) =[] s i; (3) * (0) (2); (6) []= x (5)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~(1) []= s i; (3) - (0) (2); (6) =[] x i
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.44} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $x$ and $y$ be random variables, not necessarily independent, that take real values in the interval $[0,1]$. Let $z = xy$ and let the mean values of $x, y, z$ be $\overline{x}, \overline{y}, \overline{z}$, respectively. Which one of the following statements is TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$\overline{z} = \overline{x}\overline{y}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$\overline{z} \le \overline{x}\overline{y}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$\overline{z} \ge \overline{x}\overline{y}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$\overline{z} \le \overline{x}$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.45} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The relation schema, Person(pid, city), describes the city of residence for every person uniquely identified by pid. The following relational algebra operators are available: selection, projection, cross product, and rename.
To find the list of cities where at least 3 persons reside, what is the minimum number of cross product operations required? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~1 & \textbf{(B)}~2 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~3 & \textbf{(D)}~4
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.46} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a multi-threaded program with two threads T1 and T2. The threads share two semaphores: s1 (initialized to 1) and s2 (initialized to 0). The threads also share a global variable x (initialized to 0). The threads execute the code shown below:
\begin{verbatim}
// code of T1        // code of T2
wait(s1);            wait(s1);
x = x + 1;           x = x + 1;
print(x);            print(x);
wait(s2);            signal(s2);
signal(s1);          signal(s1);
\end{verbatim}
Which of the following outcomes is/are possible when threads T1 and T2 execute concurrently? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~T1 runs first and prints 1, T2 runs next and prints 2\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~T2 runs first and prints 1, T1 runs next and prints 2\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~T1 runs first and prints 1, T2 does not print anything (deadlock)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~T2 runs first and prints 1, T1 does not print anything (deadlock)
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.47} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $A$ be an $n \times n$ matrix over the set of all real numbers $\mathbb{R}$. Let $B$ be a matrix obtained from $A$ by swapping two rows. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The determinant of $B$ is the negative of the determinant of $A$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~If $A$ is invertible, then $B$ is also invertible\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~If $A$ is symmetric, then $B$ is also symmetric\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~If the trace of $A$ is zero, then the trace of $B$ is also zero
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.48} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let S1 and S2 be two stacks. S1 has capacity of 4 elements. S2 has capacity of 2 elements. S1 already has 4 elements: 100, 200, 300, and 400 (top), whereas S2 is empty.
Only the following three operations are available: PushToS2, PushToS1, and GenerateOutput.
Which of the following output sequences can be generated? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~100, 200, 400, 300 & \textbf{(B)}~200, 300, 400, 100 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~400, 200, 100, 300 & \textbf{(D)}~300, 200, 400, 100
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.49} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which of the following is/are EQUAL to 224 in radix-5 (i.e., base-5) notation? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~64 in radix-10 & \textbf{(B)}~100 in radix-8 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~50 in radix-16 & \textbf{(D)}~121 in radix-7
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.51} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $G$ be an undirected connected graph in which every edge has a positive integer weight. Suppose that every spanning tree in $G$ has even weight. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE for every such graph $G$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~All edges in $G$ have even weight\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~All edges in $G$ have even weight OR all edges in $G$ have odd weight\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~In each cycle $C$ in $G$, all edges in $C$ have even weight\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~In each cycle $C$ in $G$, either all edges in $C$ have even weight OR all edges in $C$ have odd weight
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.52} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a context-free grammar $G$ with the following 3 rules:
\[ S \to aS \mid aSbS \mid c \]
Let $w \in L(G)$. Let $n_a(w), n_b(w), n_c(w)$ denote the number of times $a, b, c$ occur in $w$, respectively. Which of the following statements is/are TRUE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$n_a(w) > n_b(w)$ & \textbf{(B)}~$n_a(w) > n_c(w) - 2$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$n_c(w) = n_b(w) + 1$ & \textbf{(D)}~$n_c(w) = n_b(w) * 2$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.53} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a disk with the following specifications: rotation speed of 6000 RPM, average seek time of 5 milliseconds, 500 sectors/track, 512-byte sectors. A file has content stored in 3000 sectors located randomly on the disk. Assuming average rotational latency, the total time (in seconds, rounded off to 2 decimal places) to read the entire file from the disk is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in range 29.50 to 30.50) \par}

Assuming that all the segments transmitted in the next two RTTs (Round Trip Time) are acknowledged correctly, the congestion window size (in MSS) during the third RTT will be \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}

\noindent\textbf{Q.55} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider an Ethernet segment with a transmission speed of $10^8$ bits/sec and a maximum segment length of 500 meters. If the speed of propagation of the signal in the medium is $2 \times 10^8$ meters/sec, then the minimum frame size (in bits) required for collision detection is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.56} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A functional dependency $F: X \to Y$ is termed as a useful functional dependency if and only if it satisfies all the following three conditions:
\begin{itemize}
\item $X$ is not the empty set.
\item $Y$ is not the empty set.
\item Intersection of $X$ and $Y$ is the empty set.
\end{itemize}
For a relation $R$ with 4 attributes, the total number of possible useful functional dependencies is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.57} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A processor with 16 general purpose registers uses a 32-bit instruction format. The instruction format consists of an opcode field, an addressing mode field, two register operand fields, and a 16-bit scalar field. If 8 addressing modes are to be supported, the maximum number of unique opcodes possible for every addressing mode is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.58} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A non-pipelined instruction execution unit operating at 2 GHz takes an average of 6 cycles to execute an instruction of a program P. The unit is then redesigned to operate on a 5-stage pipeline at 2 GHz. Assume that the ideal throughput of the pipelined unit is 1 instruction per cycle. In the execution of program P, 20\% instructions incur an average of 2 cycles stall due to data hazards and 20\% instructions incur an average of 3 cycles stall due to control hazards. The speedup (rounded off to one decimal place) obtained by the pipelined design over the non-pipelined design is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in range 2.9 to 3.1) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.60} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The chromatic number of a graph is the minimum number of colours used in a proper colouring of the graph. The chromatic number of the given bipartite graph is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.61} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A processor uses a 32-bit instruction format and supports byte-addressable memory access. The ISA of the processor has 150 distinct instructions. The instructions are equally divided into two types, namely R-type and I-type, whose formats are shown below:
\begin{verbatim}
R-type: OPCODE | UNUSED | DST Register | SRC Register1 | SRC Register 2
I-type: OPCODE | DST Register | SRC Register | Immediate value/address
\end{verbatim}
In the OPCODE, 1 bit distinguishes between I-type and R-type and the remaining bits indicate the operation. The processor has 50 architectural registers, and all register fields are of equal size.
Let $X$ be the bits for UNUSED, $Y$ for OPCODE, and $Z$ for immediate value/address. The value of $X + 2Y + Z$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.62} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $L_1$ be the language represented by the regular expression $b^* a b^* (a b^* a b^*)^*$ and $L_2 = \{ w \in (a + b)^* \mid |w| \le 4 \}$, where $|w|$ denotes the length of string $w$. The number of strings in $L_2$ which are also in $L_1$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.63} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $Z_n$ be the group of integers $\{0, 1, 2, \dots, n-1\}$ with addition modulo $n$ as the group operation. The number of elements in the group $Z_2 \times Z_3 \times Z_4$ that are their own inverses is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.64} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a 32-bit system with 4 KB page size and page table entries of size 4 bytes each. Assume $1\text{ KB} = 2^{10}$ bytes. The OS uses a 2-level page table for memory management, with an outer page directory and an inner page table. The OS allocates 1 page for the outer page directory upon process creation. The OS uses demand paging when allocating memory for the inner page table. An active process accesses 2000 unique pages during its execution. Let $X$ denote the minimum and $Y$ denote the maximum number of pages across the two levels of the page table of the process. The value of $X + Y$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.65} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following augmented grammar, which is to be parsed with a SLR parser. The set of terminals is $\{a,b,c,d,\#,@\}$:
\[ S' \to S \]
\[ S \to SS \mid Aa \mid bAc \mid Bc \mid bBa \]
\[ A \to d\# \]
\[ B \to @ \]
Let $I_0 = \text{CLOSURE}(\{S' \to \bullet S\})$. The number of items in the set $\text{GOTO}(I_0, S)$ is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}

\subsection*{OFFICIAL ANSWER KEY --- GATE 2024 (CS2)}
\vspace{-4pt}
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{Official final answer keys published by the GATE Organising Institute. For NAT questions, allowable numerical ranges are displayed where applicable. MTA = Marks To All.}}
\vspace{4pt}
\begin{center}
\small
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
\begin{tabular}{|c|c|l||c|c|l||c|c|l||c|c|l|}
\hline

\noindent\textbf{\large\underline{GENERAL APTITUDE}}\\[2pt]
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{[Section Block: 1-mark questions]}}
\vspace{4pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.1} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Ravi had \_\_\_\_\_\_ younger brother who taught at \_\_\_\_\_\_ university. He was widely regarded as \_\_\_\_\_\_ honorable man.
Select the option with the correct sequence of articles to fill in the blanks. \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~a; a; an & \textbf{(B)}~the; an; a \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~a; an; a & \textbf{(D)}~an; an; a
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.2} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The CEO's decision to downsize the workforce was considered myopic because it sacrificed long-term stability to accommodate short-term gains.
Select the most appropriate option that can replace the word ``myopic'' without changing the meaning of the sentence. \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~visionary & \textbf{(B)}~shortsighted \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~progressive & \textbf{(D)}~innovative
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.3} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The average marks obtained by a class in an examination were calculated as 30.8. However, while checking the marks entered, the teacher found that the marks of one student were entered incorrectly as 24 instead of 42. After correcting the marks, the average becomes 31.4. How many students does the class have? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~25 & \textbf{(B)}~28 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~30 & \textbf{(D)}~32
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.4} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the relationships among P, Q, R, S, and T:
\begin{itemize}
\item P is the brother of Q.
\item S is the daughter of Q.
\item T is the sister of S.
\item R is the mother of Q.
\end{itemize}
The following statements are made based on the relationships given above:
(1) R is the grandmother of S.
(2) P is the uncle of S and T.
(3) R has only one son.
(4) Q has only one daughter.
Which one of the following options is correct? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Both (1) and (2) are true.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Both (1) and (3) are true.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Only (3) is true.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Only (4) is true.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\vspace{6pt}
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{[Section Block: 2-mark questions]}}
\vspace{3pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.6} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries ``I put the brown paper in my pocket along with the chalks, and possibly other things. I suppose every one must have reflected how primeval and how poetical are the things that one carries in one's pocket: the pocket-knife, for instance the type of all human tools, the infant of the sword. Once I planned to write a book of poems entirely about the things in my pocket. But I found it would be too long: and the age of the great epics is past.''
(From G.K. Chesterton's ``A Piece of Chalk'')
\par\smallskip
Based only on the information provided in the above passage, which one of the following statements is true? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The author of the passage carries a mirror in his pocket to reflect upon things.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~The author of the passage had decided to write a poem on epics.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The pocket-knife is described as the infant of the sword.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Epics are described as too inconvenient to write.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.8} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A fair six-faced dice, with the faces labelled `1', `2', `3', `4', `5', and `6', is rolled thrice. What is the probability of rolling `6' exactly once? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$\frac{75}{216}$ & \textbf{(B)}~$\frac{1}{6}$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$\frac{1}{18}$ & \textbf{(D)}~$\frac{25}{216}$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.10} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A shop has 4 distinct flavors of ice-cream. One can purchase any number of scoops of any flavor. The order in which the scoops are purchased is inconsequential. If one wants to purchase 3 scoops of ice-cream, in how many ways can one make that purchase? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~4 & \textbf{(B)}~20 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~24 & \textbf{(D)}~48
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

\vspace{8pt}
\noindent\textbf{\large\underline{COMPUTER SCIENCE AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY}}\\[2pt]
\noindent{\footnotesize\textit{[Section Block: 1-mark questions]}}
\vspace{4pt}

\noindent\textbf{Q.11} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Suppose a program is running on a non-pipelined single processor computer system. The computer is connected to an external device that can interrupt the processor asynchronously. The processor needs to execute the interrupt service routine (ISR) to serve this interrupt. The following steps (not necessarily in order) are taken by the processor when the interrupt arrives:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The processor saves the content of the program counter.
\item The program counter is loaded with the start address of the ISR.
\item The processor finishes the present instruction.
\end{enumerate}
Which ONE of the following is the CORRECT sequence of steps? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~(iii), (i), (ii) & \textbf{(B)}~(i), (iii), (ii) \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~(i), (ii), (iii) & \textbf{(D)}~(iii), (ii), (i)
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.12} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which ONE of the following statements is FALSE regarding the symbol table? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Symbol table is responsible for keeping track of the scope of variables.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Symbol table can be implemented using a binary search tree.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Symbol table is not required after the parsing phase.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Symbol table is created during the lexical analysis phase.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.13} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which ONE of the following techniques used in compiler code optimization uses live variable analysis? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Run-time function call management\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Register assignment to variables\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Strength reduction\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Constant folding
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.14} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a demand paging memory management system with 32-bit logical address, 20-bit physical address, and page size of 2048 bytes. Assuming that the memory is byte addressable, what is the maximum number of entries in the page table? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$2^{21}$ & \textbf{(B)}~$2^{20}$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$2^{22}$ & \textbf{(D)}~$2^{24}$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.15} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A schedule of three database transactions $T_1, T_2$, and $T_3$ is shown. $R_i(A)$ and $W_i(A)$ denote read and write of data item $A$ by transaction $T_i$, $i= 1,2,3$. The transaction $T_1$ aborts at the end. Which other transaction(s) will be required to be rolled back?
\[ R\_1(X) \; W\_1(Y) \; R\_2(X) \; R\_2(Y) \; R\_3(Y) \; ABORT(T\_1) \] \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~Only $T_2$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~Only $T_3$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Both $T_2$ and $T_3$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Neither $T_2$ nor $T_3$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.16} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Identify the ONE CORRECT matching between the OSI layers and their corresponding functionalities as shown.
\par\smallskip
\begin{tabular}{ll}
\textbf{OSI Layers} & \textbf{Functionalities} \\
(a) Network layer & (I) Packet routing \\
(b) Transport layer & (II) Framing and error handling \\
(c) Datalink layer & (III) Host to host communication
\end{tabular} \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~(a)-(I), (b)-(II), (c)-(III)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~(a)-(I), (b)-(III), (c)-(II)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~(a)-(II), (b)-(I), (c)-(III)\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~(a)-(III), (b)-(II), (c)-(I)
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.17} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries $g(\cdot)$ is a function from $A$ to $B$, $f(\cdot)$ is a function from $B$ to $C$, and their composition defined as $f(g(\cdot))$ is a mapping from $A$ to $C$.
If $f(\cdot)$ and $f(g(\cdot))$ are onto (surjective) functions, which ONE of the following is TRUE about the function $g(\cdot)$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$g(\cdot)$ must be an onto (surjective) function.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$g(\cdot)$ must be a one-to-one (injective) function.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$g(\cdot)$ must be a bijective function, that is, both one-to-one and onto.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$g(\cdot)$ is not required to be a one-to-one or onto function.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.18} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $G$ be any undirected graph with positive edge weights, and $T$ be a minimum spanning tree of $G$. For any two vertices, $u$ and $v$, let $d_1(u, v)$ and $d_2(u, v)$ be the shortest distances between $u$ and $v$ in $G$ and $T$, respectively. Which ONE of the options is CORRECT for all possible $G, T, u$ and $v$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$d_1(u, v) = d_2(u, v)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$d_1(u, v) \le d_2(u, v)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$d_1(u, v) \ge d_2(u, v)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$d_1(u, v) \ne d_2(u, v)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.19} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following context-free grammar $G$, where $S, A$, and $B$ are the variables (non-terminals), $a$ and $b$ are the terminal symbols, $S$ is the start variable, and the rules of $G$ are described as:
\[ S \to aaB \mid Abb \]
\[ A \to a \mid aA \]
\[ B \to b \mid bB \]
Which ONE of the languages $L(G)$ is accepted by $G$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$L(G) = \{a^2b^n \mid n \ge 1\} \cup \{a^nb^2 \mid n \ge 1\}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$L(G) = \{a^nb^{2n} \mid n \ge 1\} \cup \{a^{2n}b^n \mid n \ge 1\}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$L(G) = \{a^nb^n \mid n \ge 1\}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$L(G) = \{a^{2n}b^{2n} \mid n \ge 1\}$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.20} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the following recurrence relation:
\[ T(n) = 2T(n-1) + n2^n \quad \text{for } n > 0, \; T(0) = 1. \]
Which ONE of the following options is CORRECT? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n^2 2^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$T(n) = \Theta(n 2^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$T(n) = \Theta((\log n)^2 2^n)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$T(n) = \Theta(4^n)$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.21} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a $B^+$ tree with 5 nodes, in which a node can store at most 3 key values. The value 23 is now inserted in the $B^+$ tree. Which of the following option(s) is/are CORRECT? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~None of the nodes will split.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~At least one node will split and redistribute.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The total number of nodes will remain same.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~The height of the tree will increase.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.22} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the 3-way handshaking protocol for TCP connection establishment. Let the three packets exchanged during the connection establishment be denoted as P1, P2, and P3, in order. Which of the following option(s) is/are TRUE with respect to TCP header flags that are set in the packets? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~P3: SYN = 1, ACK = 1 & \textbf{(B)}~P2: SYN = 1, ACK = 1 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~P2: SYN = 0, ACK = 1 & \textbf{(D)}~P1: SYN = 1
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.23} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider the given system of linear equations for variables $x$ and $y$, where $k$ is a real-valued constant. Which of the following option(s) is/are CORRECT?
\[ x + ky = 1 \]
\[ kx + y = -1 \] \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~There is exactly one value of $k$ for which the above system of equations has no solution.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~There exist an infinite number of values of $k$ for which the system of equations has no solution.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~There exists exactly one value of $k$ for which the system of equations has exactly one solution.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~There exists exactly one value of $k$ for which the system of equations has an infinite number of solutions.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.24} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $X$ be a 3-variable Boolean function that produces output as `1' when at least two of the input variables are `1'. Which of the following statement(s) is/are CORRECT, where $a, b, c, d, e$ are Boolean variables? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$X(a, b, X(c, d, e)) = X(X(a, b, c), d, e)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$X(a, b, X(a, b, c)) = X(a, b, c)$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$X(a, b, X(a, c, d)) = (X(a, b, a) \text{ AND } X(c, d, c))$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$X(a, b, c) = X(a, X(a, b, c), X(a, c, c))$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.25} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The number $-6$ can be represented as $1010_2$ in 4-bit 2's complement representation. Which of the following is/are CORRECT 2's complement representation(s) of $-6$? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~1000 1010 in 8-bits\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~1111 1010 in 8-bits\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~1000 0000 0000 1010 in 16-bits\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~1111 1111 1111 1010 in 16-bits
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.26} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Which of the following statement(s) is/are TRUE for any binary search tree (BST) having $n$ distinct integers? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The maximum length of a path from the root node to any other node is $(n-1)$.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~An inorder traversal will always produce a sorted sequence of elements.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~Finding an element takes $O(\log_2 n)$ time in the worst case.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Every BST is also a Min-Heap.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.27} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A partial data path of a processor is given, where RA, RB, and RZ are 32-bit registers. Multiplexer Mux\_A selects between RA and a 32-bit immediate value, and Mux\_B selects between RB and a 32-bit immediate value. The output of the ALU is stored into RZ. Which option(s) is/are CORRECT related to arithmetic operations using the data path as shown? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~The data path can implement arithmetic operations involving two registers.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~The data path can implement arithmetic operations involving one register and one immediate value.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~The data path can implement arithmetic operations involving two immediate values.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~The data path can only implement arithmetic operations involving one register and one immediate value.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.28} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MSQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A regular language $L$ is accepted by a non-deterministic finite automaton (NFA) with $n$ states. Which of the following statement(s) is/are FALSE? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$L$ may have an accepting NFA with $< n$ states.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$L$ may have an accepting DFA with $< n$ states.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~There exists a DFA with $\le 2^n$ states that accepts $L$.\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~Every DFA that accepts $L$ has $> 2^n$ states.
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.29} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Suppose in a multiprogramming environment, the following C program segment is executed. A process goes into I/O queue whenever an I/O related operation is performed. Assume that there will always be a context switch whenever a process requests for an I/O, and also whenever the process returns from an I/O. The number of times the process will enter the ready queue during its lifetime (not counting the time the process enters the ready queue when it is run initially) is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer)
\par\smallskip
\begin{verbatim}
int main()
{
    int x=0, i=0;
    scanf("%d", &x);
    for(i=0; i<20; i++)
    {
        x = x + 20;
        printf("%d\n", x);
    }
    return 0;
}
\end{verbatim} \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.30} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Let $S$ be the set of all ternary strings defined over the alphabet $\{a, b, c\}$. Consider all strings in $S$ that contain at least one occurrence of two consecutive symbols, that is, ``aa'', ``bb'' or ``cc''. The number of such strings of length 5 that are possible is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}

If the function is differentiable everywhere, the value of $b$ must be \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (rounded off to one decimal place) \par}

You pick a coin at random and toss it twice, and get two heads. The probability that the coin you have chosen is the fake coin is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (rounded off to two decimal places) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.33} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[NAT]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries The pseudocode of a function fun() is given below:
\begin{verbatim}
fun(int A[0,...,n-1]){
    for i=0 to n-2
        for j=0 to n-i-2
            if (A[j] > A[j+1])
                then swap A[j] and A[j+1]
}
\end{verbatim}
Let $A[0, \ldots, 29]$ be an array storing 30 distinct integers in descending order. The number of swap operations that will be performed, if the function fun() is called with $A[0, \ldots, 29]$ as argument, is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}
\par\vspace{3pt}\noindent\textbf{Answer:} \rule{3.2cm}{0.4pt}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}

}

    int a = 20, b = 25;
    z = &a;
    foo(z, b);
    printf("%d", a);
    return 0;
}
\end{verbatim}
The output of the given C program is \_\_\_\_\_\_\. (Answer in integer) \par}

\noindent\textbf{Q.36} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider a memory system with 1M bytes of main memory and 16K bytes of cache memory. Assume that the processor generates 20-bit memory address, and the cache block size is 16 bytes. If the cache uses direct mapping, how many bits will be required to store all the tag values? [Assume memory is byte addressable, $1\text{K}=2^{10}, 1\text{M}=2^{20}$.] \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~$6 \times 2^{10}$ & \textbf{(B)}~$8 \times 2^{10}$ \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~$2^{12}$ & \textbf{(D)}~$2^{14}$
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.37} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A processor has 64 general-purpose registers and 50 distinct instruction types. An instruction is encoded in 32-bits. What is the maximum number of bits that can be used to store the immediate operand for the given instruction?
\begin{verbatim}
ADD R1, #25 // R1 = R1 + 25
\end{verbatim} \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}

\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~22 & \textbf{(D)}~24
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.38} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries A computer has two processors, $M_1$ and $M_2$. Four processes $P_1, P_2, P_3, P_4$ with CPU bursts of 20, 16, 25, and 10 milliseconds, respectively, arrive at the same time and these are the only processes in the system. The scheduler uses non-preemptive priority scheduling, with priorities decided as follows:
\begin{itemize}
\item $M_1$ uses priority of execution for the processes as, $P_1 > P_3 > P_2 > P_4$, i.e., $P_1$ and $P_4$ have highest and lowest priorities, respectively.
\item $M_2$ uses priority of execution for the processes as, $P_2 > P_3 > P_4 > P_1$, i.e., $P_2$ and $P_1$ have highest and lowest priorities, respectively.
\end{itemize}
A process $P_i$ is scheduled to a processor $M_k$, if the processor is free and no other process $P_j$ is waiting with higher priority. At any given point of time, a process can be allocated to any one of the free processors without violating the execution priority rules. Ignore the context switch time. What will be the average waiting time of the processes in milliseconds? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\begin{tabular*}{\columnwidth}{@{\extracolsep{\fill}}p{0.46\columnwidth}p{0.46\columnwidth}@{}}
\textbf{(A)}~9.00 & \textbf{(B)}~8.75 \\
\addlinespace[2pt]
\textbf{(C)}~6.50 & \textbf{(D)}~7.50
\end{tabular*}
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.39} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}
{\bfseries Consider two relations describing $teams$ and $players$ in a sports league:
\begin{itemize}
\item $teams(tid, tname)$: $tid, tname$ are team-id and team-name, respectively
\item $players(pid, pname, tid)$: $pid, pname$, and $tid$ denote player-id, player-name and the team-id of the player, respectively
\end{itemize}
Which ONE of the following tuple relational calculus queries returns the name of the players who play for the team having $tname$ as 'MI'? \par}
\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(A)}~$\{ p.pname \mid p \in players \land \exists t (t \in teams \land p.tid = t.tid \land t.tname = \text{'MI'}) \}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(B)}~$\{ p.pname \mid p \in teams \land \exists t (t \in players \land p.tid = t.tid \land t.tname = \text{'MI'}) \}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(C)}~$\{ p.pname \mid p \in players \land \exists t (t \in teams \land t.tname = \text{'MI'}) \}$\par\vspace{2pt}\noindent
\textbf{(D)}~$\{ p.pname \mid p \in teams \land \exists t (t \in players \land t.tname = \text{'MI'}) \}$
\par\vspace{6pt}\hrule height 0.25pt\vspace{6pt}
\noindent\textbf{Q.40} \hfill {\footnotesize\textsf{[MCQ]}}
\par\vspace{2pt}

145.36.255.0 & /24 & E4 \\
